An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary:
Identify customers whose rentals are overdue as of the given current date.
Algorithm:
1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
2. Create an empty list of customers.
3. Iterate through every Rental in rentals.
4. For each rental, check whether returnDate is empty and currentDate is later than dueDate.
5. If the rental is overdue, add its customer to the result list if that customer is not already included.
6. Return the list of overdue customers.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input:
- currentDate: the date used as the reference for overdue checking.
- Must be an EDate value representing a valid calendar date, or null.
Output:
- Returns a List<Customer> containing customers with at least one overdue rental.
- Returns an empty list if currentDate is null or if no overdue rentals exist.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized and accessible.
- currentDate is either null or a valid EDate.
- Each Rental has a valid dueDate and customer; returnDate may be empty.
Post-condition:
- No store data is modified.
- Every returned customer has at least one rental whose returnDate is empty and whose dueDate is earlier than currentDate.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary:
Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the results as a customer-to-count mapping.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty map from Customer to integer count.
2. Iterate through every Rental in rentals.
3. For each rental, obtain its customer.
4. If the customer is not yet in the map, insert it with count 1; otherwise increment the existing count.
5. Return the completed map.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- The calculation uses the store's current rental records.
Output:
- Returns an EMap that maps each Customer to the number of Rental records associated with that customer.
- Returns an empty map when the store has no rental records.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized and accessible.
- Each Rental in rentals has a defined customer reference.
Post-condition:
- No store data is modified.
- The returned map contains one entry per customer appearing in rentals, with values equal to their rental counts.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary:
Compute the average daily price of all cars stored in the store.
Algorithm:
1. If cars is empty, return 0.0.
2. Initialize sum to 0.0.
3. Iterate through every Car in cars.
4. Add each car's dailyPrice to sum.
5. Compute average = sum / size of cars.
6. Return average.
Related Features:
- cars
Related Classes: Car
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- The calculation uses the store's current car records.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the arithmetic mean of all car dailyPrice values.
- Returns 0.0 when the store has no car records.
Pre-condition:
- cars is initialized and accessible.
- Each Car in cars has a defined dailyPrice value.
Post-condition:
- No store data is modified.
- The returned value is the average of all stored cars' dailyPrice values, or 0.0 if none exist.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary:
Calculate the total rental revenue accumulated by all rental records stored in the store.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize revenue to 0.0.
2. Iterate through every Rental in rentals.
3. Add each rental's totalPrice to the revenue accumulator.
4. Return the accumulated revenue.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- The calculation uses the store's current rental records.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the sum of totalPrice over all rental records.
- If there are no rental records, returns 0.0.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized and accessible.
- Each Rental in rentals has a defined totalPrice value.
Post-condition:
- No store data is modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of all rental totalPrice values currently stored.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary:
Identify cars in the store that are currently available for rent and return them ordered by ascending daily price.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty list of available cars.
2. For each Car in cars, determine whether it is tied to any active rental in rentals.
3. A rental is active when its car matches the current car and its returnDate is empty.
4. If no active rental exists for the car, add it to the available list.
5. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
6. Return the sorted list.
Related Features:
- cars
- rentals
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Availability is determined from the store's current cars and rental records.
Output:
- Returns a List<Car> containing all currently available cars.
- The list is sorted in ascending order by Car.dailyPrice.
- Returns an empty list when no car is available.
Pre-condition:
- cars and rentals are initialized and accessible.
- Each Rental has a car reference and a returnDate that may be empty.
Post-condition:
- No store data is modified.
- Every returned car has no associated active rental record in the store.

```result
/*
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary:
Send an overdue notice to the specified customer and generate a trackable notice message/identifier for that customer.
Algorithm:
1. Validate that the input customer is not null.
2. Set the notice's customer reference to the given customer.
3. Compose a notice message using the noticeId and the customer's identifying information
   (name, surname, and/or address).
4. Return the composed notice message string.
Related Features:
- noticeId
- customer
Related Classes: Customer
Input:
- customer: the target Customer to receive the overdue notice.
- Must be a valid Customer object with at least name and surname populated; address may also be used in the notice text.
Output:
- Returns a String representing the overdue notice content or delivery confirmation.
- The returned string should contain the notice identifier so that the notice can be tracked.
Pre-condition:
- The provided customer must not be null.
- The OverdueNotice instance must already have a valid noticeId.
Post-condition:
- This OverdueNotice is associated with the given customer.
- A notice string is produced that can be used to track the overdue notice for that customer.

- This OverdueNotice is associated with the given customer.
- A notice string is produced that can be used to track the overdue notice for that customer.

  3.3 You should not change the method signature;

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars())
    {
      boolean hasActiveRental = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals())
      {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null)
        {
          hasActiveRental = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!hasActiveRental)
      {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }

    availableCars.sort((car1, car2) -> Double.compare(car1.getDailyPrice(), car2.getDailyPrice()));
    return availableCars;
    
  }

   * - No store data is modified.
   * - The returned value equals the sum of all rental totalPrice values currently stored.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalRevenue()
  {
    double revenue = 0.0;
    for (Rental rental : getRentals())
    {
      revenue += rental.getTotalPrice();
    }
    return revenue;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Identify customers whose rentals are overdue as of the given current date.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
   * 2. Create an empty list of customers.
   * 3. Iterate through every Rental in rentals.
   * 4. For each rental, check whether returnDate is empty and currentDate is later than dueDate.
   * 5. If the rental is overdue, add its customer to the result list if that customer is not already included.
   * 6. Return the list of overdue customers.
   * Related Features:
   * - rentals
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
   * Input:
   * - currentDate: the date used as the reference for overdue checking.
   * - Must be an EDate value representing a valid calendar date, or null.
   * Output:
   * - Returns a List<Customer> containing customers with at least one overdue rental.
   * - Returns an empty list if currentDate is null or if no overdue rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - rentals is initialized and accessible.
   * - currentDate is either null or a valid EDate.
   * - Each Rental has a valid dueDate and customer; returnDate may be empty.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No store data is modified.
   * - Every returned customer has at least one rental whose returnDate is empty and whose dueDate is earlier than currentDate.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    if (currentDate == null)
    {
      return overdueCustomers;
    }

    for (Rental rental : getRentals())
    {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && rental.getDueDate() != null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate()))
      {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (customer != null && !overdueCustomers.contains(customer))
        {
          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return overdueCustomers;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Compute the average daily price of all cars stored in the store.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If cars is empty, return 0.0.
   * 2. Initialize sum to 0.0.
   * 3. Iterate through every Car in cars.
   * 4. Add each car's dailyPrice to sum.
   * 5. Compute average = sum / size of cars.
   * 6. Return average.
   * Related Features:
   * - cars
   * Related Classes: Car
   * Input:
   * - No explicit input parameters.
   * - The calculation uses the store's current car records.
   * Output:
   * - Returns a double representing the arithmetic mean of all car dailyPrice values.
   * - Returns 0.0 when the store has no car records.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - cars is initialized and accessible.
   * - Each Car in cars has a defined dailyPrice value.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No store data is modified.
   * - The returned value is the average of all stored cars' dailyPrice values, or 0.0 if none exist.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {
    if (getCars().isEmpty())
    {
      return 0.0;
    }

    double sum = 0.0;
    for (Car car : getCars())
    {
      sum += car.getDailyPrice();
    }
    return sum / getCars().size();
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the results as a customer-to-count mapping.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Create an empty map from Customer to integer count.
   * 2. Iterate through every Rental in rentals.
   * 3. For each rental, obtain its customer.
   * 4. If the customer is not yet in the map, insert it with count 1; otherwise increment the existing count.
   * 5. Return the completed map.
   * Related Features:
   * - rentals
   * Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
   * Input:
   * - No explicit input parameters.
   * - The calculation uses the store's current rental records.
   * Output:
   * - Returns an EMap that maps each Customer to the number of Rental records associated with that customer.
   * - Returns an empty map when the store has no rental records.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - rentals is initialized and accessible.
   * - Each Rental in rentals has a defined customer reference.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No store data is modified.
   * - The returned map contains one entry per customer appearing in rentals, with values equal to their rental counts.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> customerCounts = new java.util.HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals())
    {
      Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
      Integer count = customerCounts.get(customer);
      if (count == null)
      {
        customerCounts.put(customer, Integer.valueOf(1));
      }
      else
      {
        customerCounts.put(customer, Integer.valueOf(count.intValue() + 1));
      }
    }
    return customerCounts;
    
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

```text
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary:
Computes the average daily price of all cars stored in the store.
Algorithm:
1. If cars is empty, return 0.0.
2. Initialize sum := 0.0.
3. For each car in cars:
   a. Add car.dailyPrice to sum.
4. Compute average := sum / cars.size().
5. Return average.
Related Features:
- cars
Related Classes: Car
Input:
- None.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the average of all car.dailyPrice values.
- Returns 0.0 when the store has no car records.
- Value range: average >= 0.0 assuming daily prices are non-negative.
Pre-condition:
- cars is initialized (possibly empty).
Post-condition:
- The store state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the arithmetic mean of all dailyPrice values in cars, or 0.0 if cars is empty.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary:
Calculates the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store by summing the totalPrice of each rental.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize revenue := 0.0.
2. For each rental in rentals:
   a. Add rental.totalPrice to revenue.
3. Return revenue.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental
Input:
- None.
Output:
- Returns a double representing the total revenue from all rental records.
- Value range: revenue >= 0.0 assuming rental total prices are non-negative.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized (possibly empty).
Post-condition:
- The store state is not modified.
- The returned value equals the sum of totalPrice for all rentals in the store.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary:
Identifies cars that are currently available in the store, meaning they are not tied to any active rental whose return date is still empty, and returns them sorted by ascending daily price.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty list availableCars.
2. For each car in cars:
   a. Check whether there exists any rental in rentals such that:
      - rental.car equals this car, and
      - rental.returnDate is empty.
   b. If no such active rental exists, add the car to availableCars.
3. Sort availableCars by car.dailyPrice in ascending order.
4. Return availableCars.
Related Features:
- cars
- rentals
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input:
- None.
Output:
- Returns a List<Car> of currently available cars.
- The returned list is sorted in ascending order of dailyPrice.
- Returns an empty list when no cars are available.
Pre-condition:
- cars and rentals are initialized (possibly empty).
- A rental with empty returnDate represents an active rental.
Post-condition:
- The store state is not modified.
- Every returned car has no active rental in rentals.
- Returned cars are ordered by nondecreasing dailyPrice.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary:
Lists customers whose rentals are overdue, i.e., rentals whose returnDate is empty and whose dueDate is earlier than the given currentDate.
Algorithm:
1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
2. Create an empty list overdueCustomers.
3. For each rental in rentals:
   a. If rental.returnDate is empty and currentDate is later than rental.dueDate:
      i. Add rental.customer to overdueCustomers if not already included.
4. Return overdueCustomers.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input:
- currentDate: the date used as "today" for overdue evaluation.
- Type: EDate.
- Must not be null.
Output:
- Returns a List<Customer> containing customers with at least one overdue rental.
- Returns an empty list when currentDate is null or when no overdue customers exist.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized (possibly empty).
- currentDate is either null or a valid EDate instance.
- For each rental, dueDate and returnDate are valid or empty according to the model.
Post-condition:
- The store state is not modified.
- Every returned customer corresponds to at least one rental satisfying:
  returnDate is empty AND currentDate > dueDate.
- Each customer appears at most once in the returned list.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary:
Counts how many rental records belong to each customer and returns a mapping from customer to rental count.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty map customerCount : EMap.
2. For each rental in rentals:
   a. Let c := rental.customer.
   b. If c is not in customerCount, initialize customerCount[c] := 0.
   c. Increment customerCount[c] by 1.
3. Return customerCount.
Related Features:
- rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- Returns an EMap mapping each Customer to the number of rental records associated with that customer.
- Returns an empty map when there are no rental records.
Pre-condition:
- rentals is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each rental has a non-null customer reference.
Post-condition:
- The store state is not modified.
- The returned map contains one entry per customer appearing in rentals, with counts equal to the number of rentals for that customer.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

```java
   * Summary:
   * Identifies cars that are currently available in the store, meaning they are not tied to any active rental whose return date is still empty, and returns them sorted by ascending daily price.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Create an empty list availableCars.
   * 2. For each car in cars:
   *    a. Check whether there exists any rental in rentals such that:
   *       - rental.car equals this car, and
   *       - rental.returnDate is empty.
   *    b. If no such active rental exists, add the car to availableCars.
   * 3. Sort availableCars by car.dailyPrice in ascending order.
   * 4. Return availableCars.
   * Related Features:
   * - cars
   * - rentals
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:

   * - The returned list is sorted in ascending order of dailyPrice.
   * - Returns an empty list when no cars are available.

   * - A rental with empty returnDate represents an active rental.
   * Post-condition:
   * - The store state is not modified.
```

   * @generated NOT

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars())
    {
      boolean hasActiveRental = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals())
      {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null)
        {
          hasActiveRental = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!hasActiveRental)
      {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    availableCars.sort(new Comparator<Car>()
    {
      @Override
      public int compare(Car c1, Car c2)
      {
        return Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice());
      }
    });
    return availableCars;
    
  }

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();
    if (currentDate == null)
    {
      return overdueCustomers;
    }
    for (Rental rental : getRentals())
    {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate()))
      {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (!overdueCustomers.contains(customer))

          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return overdueCustomers;

  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {

    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double sum = 0.0;

  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()

      Integer count = customerCount.get(customer);

        count = Integer.valueOf(0);
      }
      customerCount.put(customer, Integer.valueOf(count.intValue() + 1));
    }
    return customerCount;
    
  }
